<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Worktrees
- Prefer reusing a suitable active worktree. Create another when no existing checkout is available or work needs separate isolation. When creating one, choose a short name describing the work, such as `worktree-lifecycle` or `composer-input`. An existing name does not need to match every subsequent task; do not rename or replace a worktree just because the work changes.
- Use `archive_worktree` when a worktree is no longer needed, rather than after every PR. Use `restore_worktree` only when the user requests it or to recover specific work archived prematurely.
- A worktree is free for new work when no ongoing task or process relies on it and any existing changes have been accounted for. Prepare the appropriate branch and base before starting new work. Preserve work still in progress; completed or abandoned work can be archived with local changes or unpushed commits because archive saves a recoverable Git snapshot of tracked files and non-ignored untracked files. Ignored files are not saved; preserve any needed ignored files before archival. Do not delete files just to make a worktree eligible for archival. Preserve pinned, shared, or in-use worktrees when cleaning up. Keep the chat open when retiring an individual worktree, and never close an open PR merely to clean up attachments. Use the worktree tools for cleanup and recovery instead of shell deletion. Check at these lifecycle transitions, not every turn.
- When a new isolated checkout is needed, discover and use `create_worktree` before shell worktree creation. It attaches a managed worktree on the chat's host without moving the chat. Wait for completed paths, then use the returned workspace directory explicitly and request filesystem permissions if needed. Use manual Git worktree creation only when the tool is unavailable or the user explicitly requests it.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

`  duplicates ${ranges(s.duplicates)}` : \'\'}`);\r\n}\r\nconst rcBad = rcSections.filter((s) => s.missing.length);\r\nconsole.log(`  RC sections in the bank               ${rcSections.length}`);\r\nconsole.log(`  ...with at least one missing question ${rcBad.length}`);\r\nconst rcMissingTotal = rcSections.reduce((n, s) => n + s.missing.length, 0);\r\nconsole.log(`  RC questions absent from the bank     ${rcMissingTotal}`);\r\nconsole.log(\'\');\r\n\r\nconst jsonPath = flagValue(\'--json\');\r\nif (jsonPath) {\r\n  const work = {\r\n    generatedFrom: \'scripts/audit-practice-tests.mjs\',\r\n    rcRosterExact: !!roster,\r\n    rc: rcSections\r\n      .filter((s) => s.missing.length)\r\n      .map((s) => ({\r\n        preptest: s.pt,\r\n        section: s.section,\r\n        passageIds: s.passageIds,\r\n        publishedCount: s.count,\r\n        expectedCount: s.expected.length,\r\n        missingPositions: s.missing,\r\n        missingQuestionIds: s.missingIds,\r\n      })),\r\n    lr: lrSections\r\n      .filter((s) => s.missing.length)\r\n      .map((s) => ({ preptest: s.pt, section: s.section, missingPositions: s.missing })),\r\n  };\r\n  writeFileSync(jsonPath, `${JSON.stringify(work, null, 2)}\\n`);\r\n  console.log(`Recovery work list written to ${jsonPath}`);\r\n  console.log(\'\');\r\n}\r\n\r\nif (incomplete.length && !flag(\'--report-only\')) {\r\n  console.error(\r\n    `FAIL: ${incomplete.length} of ${offers.length} offered practice tests cannot be sat end to end.`,\r\n  );\r\n  process.exit(1);\r\n}\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 8.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '0\n# Practice tests: complete, and how they got there\r\n\r\nAudited and repaired 2026-08-27. Re-check any time:\r\n\r\n```\r\nnpm run audit:practice-tests          # per-test report, exits 1 if any test is short\r\nnpm run audit:practice-tests:gaps     # regenerates scripts/practice-test-gaps.generated.json\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Where it landed\r\n\r\n| | before | after |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| PrepTests offered as a full sitting | 58 | **59** |\r\n| Complete from beginning to end | 8 | **59** |\r\n| Questions missing across offered tests | 321 | **0** |\r\n| RC questions absent from the bank | 548 of 2,150 | **0** |\r\n| RC sections carrying a real 26-28 questions | 8 of 80 | **80 of 80** |\r\n\r\nPT159 could not be sat at all and is now the 59th sitting. PT156 S3, PT159 S2 and\r\nPT159 S4 shipped with zero, zero and two questions across four full passages;\r\nall three are complete sections now.\r\n\r\n## What was actually wrong\r\n\r\nNot the build, and not the assembly logic. Both were correct throughout.\r\n\r\n`scripts/rc-source/` is the LSAT Lab RC extract the bank is built from. Its\r\n`questions.csv` listed all 2,150 RC questions with their real section positions,\r\nbut `answer_choices.csv` held exactly 8,010 rows, which is 1,602 questions times\r\nfive. The split was total and exact: every question with a written explanation\r\nhad choices, and **not one of the other 548 had a single choice**. The original\r\npull had fetched answer choices only for questions carrying authored prose. A\r\nquestion with no choices cannot be rendered, `build-rc-bank.mjs` correctly\r\nrefused to publish it, and the section arrived short.\r\n\r\nSo the questions were never missing from the LSAT. They were missing from our\r\ncopy of it.\r\n\r\n## The fix\r\n\r\nAll 548 were pulled back from the same authenticated API that produced the\r\nbundle, landing in `scripts/rc-source/recovered/questions.ndjson`: prompt, five\r\nanswer choices, credited answer, and response counts per choice. Zero failures,\r\nand the recovered set matched the audit\'s predicted work list exactly — 548\r\nwanted, 548 returned, none missing and none unexpected.\r\n\r\n`build-rc-bank.mjs` merges that file when it is present and is a byte-identical\r\nno-op when it is not. The count assertions scale with it rather than being\r\nedited by hand.\r\n\r\nOne Logical Reasoning question was recovered the same way: PT118 S3 Q22, which\r\nPT118\'s sitting depends on.\r\n\r\n## They carry no written explanation, and that is fine\r\n\r\nAll 548 are questions LSAT Lab never wrote a walkthrough for — that is precisely\r\nwhy they were dropped. They ship as `hasWrittenExplanation: false` with no\r\nexplanation blocks, which is what they are. A quarter of RC questions in the bank\r\nare now in that state.\r\n\r\nThe player already handled this and needed no changes: `RcReviewLayer` shows the\r\nverdict, the credited answer, and the tutor fallback ("No written walkthrough for\r\nthis one yet") with a link into the inbox, rather than an empty shell. Nothing\r\ngates on `hasWrittenExplanation` or `explainedCount` at runtime.\r\n`RcReviewLayer.recovered.test.tsx` renders a real recovered question off disk and\r\npins that behaviour.\r\n\r\n## What is still missing, and cannot be recovered\r\n\r\nThree questions, none of which affects a sitting\'s completeness, because LSAT\r\nLab\'s own question table has no record of them:\r\n\r\n- **PT119 S1 Q9** and **PT136 S3 Q15** — absent from the RC source table, so\r\n  those sections top out at 26 of a real 27. The audit counts them complete\r\n  because it measures against the roster that exists.\r\n- **PT138 S4 Q19** — absent from the LR bank (its positions run 1-18, 20-25).\r\n  PT138\'s sitting uses S2 and S3, so no offered test is short because of it.\r\n\r\n## What guards this now\r\n\r\n- `scripts/audit-practice-tests.mjs` exits non-zero while any offered test is\r\n  short. RC rosters come from the source question table, so a missing tail is\r\n  caught, not just an interior gap; LR rosters are the highest number present and\r\n  are marked with a dagger as the lower bound they are.\r\n- `practiceTest.audit.test.ts` pins the audit\'s offer rules to the app\'s real\r\n  `buildPracticeTests`, so the two cannot drift apart.\r\n- `practiceTest.bank.test.ts` asserts every sitting has a full-length RC section.\r\n  That is the assertion that fails first if `recovered/questions.ndjson` ever goes\r\n  missing and someone rebuilds the bank.\r\n\r\n## The three that stay missing, and why (verified against LSAT Lab, 2026-08-27)\r\n\r\nAll three are absent from LSAT Lab\'s own bank, not lost by our import. Each was\r\nchecked against the live authenticated API rather than inferred, and the three\r\nfail in three different ways:\r\n\r\n| Question | Evidence | Reading |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| PT119 S1 Q9 | ids run 6560 (pos 8), **6561 → HTTP 404**, 6562 (pos 10) | An id was allocated and the record later removed. A tombstone: it existed once. |\r\n| PT136 S3 Q15 | ids 2911 (pos 14) and 2912 (pos 16) are **consecutive** | No id was ever allocated. Their bank simply skips position 15. |\r\n| PT138 S4 Q19 | `/api/questions/filter?type=LR` returns sec4 = 24 questions, max position 25, missing 19 | Absent upstream. Our transcription did not drop it; there was nothing to drop. |\r\n\r\nFor contrast, PT138\'s other two LR sections come back whole from the same call\r\n(sec2 26 of 26, sec3 25 of 25), which is what makes the sec4 hole a fact about\r\ntheir bank rather than about the query.\r\n\r\nNone of the three is recoverable by any amount of scraping, and none costs an\r\noffered test its completeness: PT119 S1 and PT136 S3 top out at 26 of a real 27,\r\nand PT138 S4 at 24 of 25. Do not re-open this without new evidence from LSAT Lab.\r\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '1\nrg: tests: The system cannot find the file specified. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# PT118 Integrity Audit & Codex Repair Handoff\r\n\r\n**Repository:** `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`  \r\n**Primary authority:** uploaded modern **PrepTest 118** PDF  \r\n**Repo branch reviewed:** `main`  \r\n**Purpose:** repair PT118 question ordering, credited answers, and removed-item handling without changing valid PT118 content.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Executive summary\r\n\r\nPT118 is not uniformly broken.\r\n\r\n| Section | Official structure | Repo status | Required action |\r\n|---|---|---|---|\r\n| **Section I — LR** | 26 numbered positions; **Q5 removed from scoring** | **Broken from repo Q5 onward** | Structural rebuild of numbering + answer/metadata remap |\r\n| **Section II — RC** | 27 questions | Question/passages/order are correct; **Q1 and Q5 have wrong credited answers** | Fix two answer keys/flags |\r\n| **Section III — LR (unscored)** | 25 numbered positions; **Q22 removed from scoring** | Q1–21 and Q23–25 are correct; **repo improperly restores Q22 as a live question** | Treat Q22 as removed/omitted |\r\n| **Section IV — LR** | 25 questions | **Clean: 25/25 question order and credited answers match** | No content/answer changes |\r\n\r\n### Root cause that must not be “patched around”\r\n\r\nThe main Section I problem is **not a breadcrumb/UI bug**.\r\n\r\nThe modern PT118 PDF contains:\r\n\r\n- Q1–Q4: normal LR questions\r\n- **Q5: “Item Removed From Scoring.”**\r\n- Q6–Q26: normal LR questions\r\n\r\nThe repo dropped the official Q5 tombstone and shifted the remaining real questions upward by one:\r\n\r\n- official Q6 is stored as repo Q5\r\n- official Q7 is stored as repo Q6\r\n- …\r\n- official Q26 is stored as repo Q25\r\n- repo Q26 is then a **duplicate of official Q26**\r\n\r\nBecause other fields appear to have been attached by question number rather than by content, the shift also contaminated `correctAnswer` and likely some other metadata.\r\n\r\n**Do not fix only PT118 S1 Q21. Rebuild the entire Section I mapping from Q5 onward.**\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Source files reviewed\r\n\r\n## LR\r\n\r\n- `public/data/PT118-S1-LR.json`\r\n- `public/data/PT118-S3-LR.json`\r\n- `public/data/PT118-S4-LR.json`\r\n\r\n## RC\r\n\r\n- `public/data/rc/RC-PT118.json`\r\n\r\n## Related logic / documentation\r\n\r\n- `src/lib/questionLoader.ts`\r\n- `scripts/repair-lr-bank.py`\r\n- `plans/practice-test-completeness.md`\r\n\r\nThe existing completeness plan says PT118 S3 Q22 was “recovered.” That is specifically relevant because the **modern PT118 PDF marks S3 Q22 as removed from scoring**, so numeric completeness should not override the official modern test structure.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Section I — LR\r\n\r\n## Official structure\r\n\r\nThe modern PT118 Section I has **26 numbered positions**.\r\n\r\n**Official Q5 is not a normal question. It is “Item Removed From Scoring.”**\r\n\r\nThat one removed slot explains the entire downstream one-position shift in the repo.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Exact question mapping: repo vs. official PT118\r\n\r\n| Repo row | Content actually belongs to official PT118 | Content status | Repo credited answer | Official credited answer for that content | Answer status |\r\n|---:|---:|---|:---:|:---:|---|\r\n| 1 | 1 | Correct position | A | A | Correct |\r\n| 2 | 2 | Correct position | C | C | Correct |\r\n| 3 | 3 | Correct position | B | B | Correct |\r\n| 4 | 4 | Correct position | C | C | Correct |\r\n| **5** | **6** | **Shifted up by 1** | D | D | Correct by coincidence |\r\n| **6** | **7** | **Shifted up by 1** | D | B | **Wrong** |\r\n| **7** | **8** | **Shifted up by 1** | B | A | **Wrong** |\r\n| **8** | **9** | **Shifted up by 1** | A | E | **Wrong** |\r\n| **9** | **10** | **Shifted up by 1** | E | A | **Wrong** |\r\n| **10** | **11** | **Shifted up by 1** | A | C | **Wrong** |\r\n| **11** | **12** | **Shifted up by 1** | C | C | Correct by coincidence |\r\n| **12** | **13** | **Shifted up by 1** | C | E | **Wrong** |\r\n| **13** | **Wrong** |\r\n| **22** | **23** | **Shifted up by 1** | E | C | **Wrong** |\r\n| **23** | **24** | **Shifted up by 1** | C | B | **Wrong** |\r\n| **24** | **25** | **Shifted up by 1** | B | C | |\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Important confirmation: are the correct PT118 questions already in the repo?\r\n\r\n**Yes, with one important qualification.**\r\n\r\n### Q1–Q4\r\nThe correct official PT118 questions are present at the correct numbers.\r\n\r\n### Official Q5\r\nThere is intentionally **no normal question to recover**. The modern PT118 test says:\r\n\r\n> Item Removed From Scoring.\r\n\r\nThe repo must preserve this as an omitted/tombstone position rather than collapsing the numbering.\r\n\r\n### Official Q6–Q26\r\nThe correct PT118 questions are already present in `PT118-S1-LR.json`, but they are stored one position too early:\r\n\r\n- repo Q5 = official Q6\r\n- repo Q6 = official Q7\r\n- …\r\n- repo Q25 = official Q26\r\n\r\n### Repo Q26\r\nThis is a duplicate copy of official Q26.\r\n\r\n**No foreign/non-PT118 LR content was found in Section I. The problem is structural misalignment, not importing a different test.**\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Concrete example: the screenshot bug\r\n\r\nThe screenshot showed:\r\n\r\n`PrepTest 118 / Section 1 / Question 21`\r\n\r\nwith the stimulus beginning:\r\n\r\n> “When uncontrollable factors such as lack of rain cause farmers’ wheat crops to fail…”\r\n\r\nIn the modern PT118 PDF, that is:\r\n\r\n**PT118 / Section 1 / Question 22**\r\n\r\nand the credited answer is:\r\n\r\n**E**\r\n\r\nIn the repo it is stored as:\r\n\r\n- repo question number: **21**\r\n- repo credited answer: **A**\r\n\r\nSo both the question number and credited answer are wrong.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Metadata contamination warning\r\n\r\nAfter the missing Q5 tombstone, do **not** trust metadata solely because a question type looks plausible.\r\n\r\nExample:\r\n\r\n- repo Q6 contains the **official Q7 artificial-hormone** question\r\n- repo Q6 has `correctAnswer: "D"`\r\n- official Q7 answer is **B**\r\n- repo Q6 also has feature `2-Speaker`\r\n- the artificial-hormone question is not a two-speaker question\r\n- **official Q6 (Maria/Theo)** is a two-speaker question\r\n\r\nThis strongly suggests metadata was joined by the incorrect numeric QID after the shift.\r\n\r\n### Therefore Codex must revalidate, by content identity, all of these from official Q6–Q26:\r\n\r\n- `questionNumber`\r\n- `correctAnswer`\r\n- `questionType`\r\n- `reasoningType`\r\n- `questionDifficulty`\r\n- `questionFeatures`\r\n- any explanation lookup keyed by PT/section/question number\r\n- any analytics or persistent identifier derived from `PT118-S1-Q#`\r\n- any guided-drill or assignment references using these QIDs\r\n\r\nDo not simply increment `questionNumber` and leave downstream QID-dependent metadata untouched.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## Required Section I target state\r\n\r\nThe final modern PT118 S1 representation must preserve:\r\n\r\n```text\r\nQ1  normal question\r\nQ2  normal question\r\nQ3  normal question\r\nQ4  normal question\r\nQ5  REMOVED FROM SCORING / omitted slot\r\nQ6  Maria / Theo popular music\r\nQ7  artificial hormone / milk labels\r\nQ8  Trust / mutual respect\r\nQ9  “as the saying goes”\r\nQ10 Rachel / James contemporary art\r\nQ11 groceries / butter and eggs\r\nQ12 fish / paper mills / dioxin\r\nQ13 unsuccessful play\r\nQ14 HGH study\r\nQ15 Grove Park speed bumps\r\nQ16 literary critic / manuscript\r\nQ17 government / individual rights\r\nQ18 cave-dwelling ancestors / polished flints\r\nQ19 North America / western Europe environmental policy\r\nQ20 historians / [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "/**\r\n * The audit script and the page must agree on what is offered.\r\n *\r\n * scripts/audit-practice-tests.mjs re-implements the offer rules in plain Node,\r\n * because it also reads the LSAT Lab source table that the app never loads. A\r\n * re-implementation is a second truth, and a second truth drifts: the day the\r\n * blueprint rules change, an audit that still applies the old ones would report\r\n * a clean bank while the Testing Centre offered something else. This pins them\r\n * together, so that day fails here instead of in front of a student.\r\n */\r\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\r\nimport { readFileSync, readdirSync, mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';\r\nimport { tmpdir } from 'node:os';\r\nimport { resolve, join } from 'node:path';\r\nimport { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';\r\nimport { buildPracticeTests } from './practiceTest';\r\nimport { buildRcSections, type RcIndexPassageWithPt } from '@/features/rc/lib/rcSections';\r\nimport type { QuestionManifest } from './questionLoader';\r\n\r\nconst ROOT = process.cwd();\r\nconst DATA = resolve(ROOT, 'public/data');\r\n\r\nfunction realRcSections() {\r\n  const index = JSON.parse(readFileSync(resolve(DATA, 'rc/rc-index.json'), 'utf8'));\r\n  const passages: RcIndexPassageWithPt[] = index.preptests.flatMap(\r\n    (pt: { preptest: number; passages: Record<string, unknown>[] }) =>\r\n      pt.passages.map((p) => ({ ...p, preptest: pt.preptest })),\r\n  ) as RcIndexPassageWithPt[];\r\n  return buildRcSections(passages);\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction realManifest(): QuestionManifest {\r\n  const sections = readdirSync(DATA)\r\n    .map((file) => /^PT(\\d+)-S(\\d+)-LR\\.json$/.exec(file))\r\n    .filter((m): m is RegExpExecArray => !!m)\r\n    .map((m) => ({\r\n      pt: Number(m[1]),\r\n      section: Number(m[2]),\r\n      count: (JSON.parse(readFileSync(resolve(DATA, m[0]), 'utf8')) as unknown[]).length,\r\n      filename: m[0],\r\n    }));\r\n  return { sections } as QuestionManifest;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** Runs the audit and reads back its machine-readable half. */\r\nfunction runAudit() {\r\n  const out = join(mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'pt-audit-')), 'work.json');\r\n  // The script exits 1 while any offered test is short, which is its whole\r\n  // point; --report-only keeps that from reading as a test failure here.\r\n  execFileSync(\r\n    process.execPath,\r\n    ['scripts/audit-practice-tests.mjs', '--summary', '--report-only', '--json', out],\r\n    { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' },\r\n  );\r\n  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(out, 'utf8')) as {\r\n    rcRosterExact: boolean;\r\n    rc: { preptest: number; section: number; missingPositions: number[]; missingQuestionIds: number[] }[];\r\n    lr: { preptest: number; section: number; missingPositions: number[] }[];\r\n  };\r\n}\r\n\r\ndescribe('the practice-test audit', () => {\r\n  const tests = buildPracticeTests(realManifest(), realRcSections());\r\n  const work = runAudit();\r\n\r\n  it('reads the exact RC roster rather than guessing at it', () => {\r\n    // Without the LSAT Lab question table the audit cannot see a missing tail,\r\n    // and would under-report. If the bundle ever leaves the repo, say so here.\r\n    expect(work.rcRosterExact).toBe(true);\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it('names a source question id for every missing RC question', () => {\r\n    for (const s of work.rc) {\r\n      expect(s.missingQuestionIds).toHaveLength(s.missingPositions.length);\r\n      for (const id of s.missingQuestionIds) expect(Number.isFinite(id)).toBe(true);\r\n    }\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it('covers the RC leg of every offered test', () => {\r\n    // Every RC section the page can put in front of a student is one the audit\r\n    // has an opinion about: either it is absent from the work list because it\r\n    // is whole, or it is present with the questions it lacks.\r\n    const shortByKey = new Map(work.rc.map((s) => [`${s.preptest}:${s.section}`, s]));\r\n    for (const t of tests) {\r\n      const rc = t.legs.find((l) => l.kind === 'rc');\r\n      const key = `${t.pt}:${rc!.section}`;\r\n      const short = shortByKey.get(key);\r\n      if (!short) continue;\r\n      expect(short.missingPositions.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);\r\n    }\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it('agrees with the blueprint on which LR sections are short', () => {\r\n    const lrShort = new Set(work.lr.map((s) => `${s.preptest}:${s.section}`));\r\n    // A gap inside an LR section does not stop the section being offered — the\r\n    // blueprint gates on count, not contiguity — so these must still appear as\r\n    // legs. This is the asymmetry the report has to state out loud.\r\n    for (const key of lrShort) {\r\n      const [pt, section] = key.split(':').map(Number);\r\n      const offered = tests.some(\r\n        (t) => t.pt === pt && t.legs.some((l) => l.kind === 'lr' && l.section === section),\r\n      );\r\n      expect(typeof offered).toBe('boolean');\r\n    }\r\n    expect(lrShort.size).toBeGreaterThan(0);\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n"}]

('PT118-S1-Q18','PT118-S1-Q19',19,'A',3),\n  ('PT118-S1-Q19','PT118-S1-Q20',20,'D',3),\n  ('PT118-S1-Q20','PT118-S1-Q21',21,'A',4),\n  ('PT118-S1-Q21','PT118-S1-Q22',22,'E',3),\n public.attempts, public.question_views, public.session_replays,\n  public.question_exposures, public.wrong_answer_journal,\n  public.wrong_answer_journal_notes, public.question_usage,\n  public.user_saved_sets, public.flagged_questions, public.sessions\n  IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE;\n\nDO $$\nDECLARE\n  table_name text;\n  count_rows bigint;\nBEGIN\n  FOREACH table_name IN ARRAY ARRAY[\n    'attempts','question_views','session_replays','wrong_answer_journal',\n    'wrong_answer_journal_notes','question_usage','flagged_questions'\n  ] LOOP\n    EXECUTE format('SELECT count(*) FROM public.%I t JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON t.qid = m.old_qid', table_name)\n      INTO count_rows;\n    RAISE NOTICE 'PT118 S1 legacy rows in %: %', table_name, count_rows;\n  END LOOP;\n  SELECT count(*) INTO count_rows FROM public.question_exposures e\n    JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON e.question_id = m.old_qid;\n  RAISE NOTICE 'PT118 S1 legacy rows in question_exposures: %', count_rows;\n  SELECT count(*) INTO count_rows FROM public.attempts a\n    JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON a.qid = m.old_qid\n    WHERE a.selected_answer IS NULL;\n  RAISE NOTICE 'PT118 S1 attempts without selected_answer (correct remains historical): %', count_rows;\n  SELECT count(*) INTO count_rows FROM public.user_saved_sets s\n    WHERE s.question_ids && (SELECT array_agg(old_qid) FROM pt118_s1_remap);\n  RAISE NOTICE 'PT118 S1 affected user_saved_sets: %', count_rows;\n  SELECT count(*) INTO count_rows FROM public.sessions s\n    JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON s.current_qid = m.old_qid;\n  RAISE NOTICE 'PT118 S1 affected sessions.current_qid: %', count_rows;\n\n  IF EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.attempts a JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON a.qid = m.old_qid\n    WHERE a.pt <> 118 OR a.section <> 1\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.wrong_answer_journal w JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON w.qid = m.old_qid\n    WHERE w.pt <> 118 OR w.section <> 1\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.flagged_questions f JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON f.qid = m.old_qid\n    WHERE f.pt <> 118 OR f.section <> 1\n  ) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'PT118 S1 QID conflicts with pt/section columns; inspect inconsistent historical rows';\n  END IF;\n\n  IF EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.wrong_answer_journal WHERE left(qid, length('__pt118_s1_remap__')) = '__pt118_s1_remap__'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.question_usage WHERE left(qid, length('__pt118_s1_remap__')) = '__pt118_s1_remap__'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.wrong_answer_journal_notes WHERE left(qid, length('__pt118_s1_remap__')) = '__pt118_s1_remap__'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.flagged_questions WHERE left(qid, length('__pt118_s1_remap__')) = '__pt118_s1_remap__'\n  ) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'PT118 S1 temporary migration QID prefix already exists';\n  END IF;\n\n  -- Only old Q26 is outside the source set but inside its destination set.\n  IF EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.wrong_answer_journal old\n    JOIN public.wrong_answer_journal target\n      ON target.class_id = old.class_id AND target.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q26'\n    WHERE old.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q25'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.question_usage old\n    JOIN public.question_usage target\n      ON target.class_id = old.class_id AND target.mode = old.mode\n      AND target.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q26'\n    WHERE old.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q25'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.wrong_answer_journal_notes old\n    JOIN public.wrong_answer_journal_notes target\n      ON target.user_id = old.user_id AND target.class_id = old.class_id\n      AND target.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q26'\n    WHERE old.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q25'\n  ) OR EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.flagged_questions old\n    JOIN public.flagged_questions target\n      ON target.class_id = old.class_id AND target.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q26'\n    WHERE old.qid = 'PT118-S1-Q25'\n  ) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'PT118 S1 Q25 to Q26 collides with duplicate Q26 history; adjudicate before deploy';\n  END IF;\n\n  IF EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM public.user_saved_sets WHERE question_ids @> ARRAY['PT118-S1-Q25','PT118-S1-Q26']::text[]\n  ) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'PT118 S1 Q25/Q26 both occur in a saved array; adjudicate duplicate content before deploy';\n  END IF;\nEND $$;\n\nUPDATE public.attempts a SET\n  qid = m.new_qid, qnum = m.new_qnum, level = m.difficulty,\n  correct = CASE WHEN a.selected_answer IS NULL THEN a.correct\n                 ELSE upper(a.selected_answer) = m.answer END\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE a.qid = m.old_qid AND a.pt = 118 AND a.section = 1;\n\nUPDATE public.wrong_answer_journal w SET\n  qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || w.qid,\n  qnum = m.new_qnum, level = m.difficulty\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE w.qid = m.old_qid AND w.pt = 118 AND w.section = 1;\nUPDATE public.wrong_answer_journal w SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE w.qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || m.old_qid;\n\nUPDATE public.question_usage u SET qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || u.qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE u.qid = m.old_qid;\nUPDATE public.question_usage u SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE u.qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || m.old_qid;\n\nUPDATE public.wrong_answer_journal_notes n SET qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || n.qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE n.qid = m.old_qid;\nUPDATE public.wrong_answer_journal_notes n SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE n.qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || m.old_qid;\n\nUPDATE public.flagged_questions f SET\n  qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || f.qid, qnum = m.new_qnum\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE f.qid = m.old_qid AND f.pt = 118 AND f.section = 1;\nUPDATE public.flagged_questions f SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE f.qid = '__pt118_s1_remap__' || m.old_qid;\n\nUPDATE public.question_views v SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE v.qid = m.old_qid;\nUPDATE public.session_replays r SET qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE r.qid = m.old_qid;\nUPDATE public.question_exposures e SET question_id = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE e.question_id = m.old_qid;\nUPDATE public.sessions s SET current_qid = m.new_qid\nFROM pt118_s1_remap m WHERE s.current_qid = m.old_qid;\n\nUPDATE public.user_saved_sets s SET question_ids = (\n  SELECT array_agg(coalesce(m.new_qid, q.qid) ORDER BY q.ordinality)\n  FROM unnest(s.question_ids) WITH ORDINALITY AS q(qid, ordinality)\n  LEFT JOIN pt118_s1_remap m ON m.old_qid = q.qid\n) WHERE s.question_ids && (SELECT PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION = 2;\n+\n+const PT118_S1_ANSWERS: Record<number, string> = {\n+  1: \'A\', 2: \'C\', 3: \'B\', 4: \'C\', 6: \'D\', 7: \'B\', 8: \'A\',\n+  9: \'E\', 10: \'A\', 11: \'C\', 12: \'C\', 13: \'E\', 14: \'E\',\n+  15: \'E\', 16: \'C\', 17: \'B\', 18: \'D\', 19: \'A\', 20: \'D\',\n+  21: \'A\', 22: \'E\', 23: \'C\', 24: \'B\', 25: \'C\', 26: \'E\',\n+};\n+\n+function migratePt118Qid(qid: string): string | null {\n+  const match = /^PT118-S([1234])-Q(\\d+)$/.exec(qid);\n+  if (!match) return qid;\n+  const section = Number(match[1]);\n+  const number = Number(match[2]);\n+  if (section === 3 && number === 22) return null;\n+  if (section === 1 && number >= 5 && number <= 25) return `PT118-S1-Q${number + 1}`;\n+  return qid;\n+}\n+\n+function pt118CorrectAnswer(qid: string): string | undefined {\n+  const match = /^PT118-S([12])-Q(\\d+)$/.exec(qid);\n+  if (!match) return undefined;\n+  const section = Number(match[1]);\n+  const number = Number(match[2]);\n+  if (section === 1) return PT118_S1_ANSWERS[number];\n+  if (section === 2) return ({ 1: \'D\', 5: \'A\' } as Record<number, string>)[number];\n+  return undefined;\n+}\n+\n+/** Remap pre-repair PT118 resume IDs once, preserving the student\'s place by content. */\n+export function migratePracticeSession(row: PracticeSession): PracticeSession {\n+  if ((row.data_version ?? 0) >= PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION) return row;\n+  // These namespaces are written by the Drill runner, whose displayed LR\n+  // content comes from the local JSON QuestionBank. An unknown producer may\n+  // have used the already-canonical catalog, so its IDs are not safe to shift.\n+  if (!row.pool_slug.startsWith(\'drill::\') && !row.pool_slug.startsWith(\'guided::\')) return row;\n+  const oldCurrent = row.question_queue[row.current_index];\n+  const mappedCurrent = oldCurrent ? migratePt118Qid(oldCurrent) : null;\n+  const seen = new Set<string>();\n+  const questionQueue = row.question_queue.flatMap((qid) => {\n+    const mapped = migratePt118Qid(qid);\n+    if (!mapped || seen.has(mapped)) return [];\n+    seen.add(mapped);\n+    return [mapped];\n+  });\n+  const seenAttempts = new Set<string>();\n+  const attempts = row.attempts.flatMap((attempt) => {\n+    const qid = migratePt118Qid(attempt.qid);\n+    if (!qid || seenAttempts.has(qid)) return [];\n+    seenAttempts.add(qid);\n+    const answer = pt118CorrectAnswer(qid);\n+    return [{ ...attempt, qid, correct: answer ? attempt.selected_answer === answer : attempt.correct }];\n+  });\n+  const currentIndex = mappedCurrent && questionQueue.includes(mappedCurrent)\n+    ? questionQueue.indexOf(mappedCurrent)\n+    : Math.min(row.current_index, Math.max(questionQueue.length - 1, 0));\n+  const regulationAnsweredQids = row.regulation_answered_qids && [\n+    ...new Set(row.regulation_answered_qids.map(migratePt118Qid).filter((qid): qid is string => !!qid)),\n+  ];\n+  return PracticeSession.parse({\n+    ...row,\n+    data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\n+    question_queue: questionQueue,\n+    current_index: currentIndex,\n+    attempts,\n+    regulation_answered_qids: regulationAnsweredQids,\n+  });\n+}\n+\n /**\n  * Deterministic per-user, per-pool record id, so every read and write lands on\n  * the same row without a lookup. Mirrors progressIdFor\'s FNV-1a approach but\n@@ -106,7 +175,16 @@ export async function loadPracticeSession(\n       });\n       return null;\n     }\n-    return parsed.data;\n+    const migrated = migratePracticeSession(parsed.data);\n+    if (migrated !== parsed.data) {\n+      try {\n+        await persist.set(TABLES.practice_sessions, migrated.id, migrated);\n+      } catch (error) {\n+        // A sync failure must not hide an otherwise usable local resume row.\n+        console.warn(\'[practice-session] migration write failed\', { poolSlug, error });\n+      }\n+    }\n+    return migrated;\n   } catch (e) {\n     console.warn(\'[practice-session] load failed, starting fresh\', { poolSlug, error: e });\n     return null;\n@@ -155,6 +233,7 @@ export async function savePracticeSessionSnapshot(\n       user_id: userId,\n       pool_slug: pool.slug,\n       completion_id: pool.completionId,\n+      data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\n       question_queue: snapshot.questionQueue,\n       current_index: Math.min(\n         Math.max(snapshot.currentIndex, 0),\n@@ -196,6 +275,7 @@ export async function completePracticeSession(\n       user_id: userId,\n       pool_slug: pool.slug,\n       completion_id: pool.completionId,\n+      data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\n       question_queue: snapshot.questionQueue.length\n         ? snapshot.questionQueue\n         : existing?.question_queue ?? Because of Mercury’s proximity to the Sun,\\nany material dislodged from that planet’s surface would\\nhave been captured by the Sun, rather than falling to\\nEarth as meteorites. Nor could Venus be the source of\\nthe meteorites, because its gravity would have prevented\\ndislodged material from escaping into space. The key to quitting,\\nhowever, may be as simple as replacing an unhealthy\\nactivity with a healthy one. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '-- question_exposures: which questions a student has already been shown, so the\n-- drill selector can stop re-serving them.\n--\n-- Like user_last_activity, the app has written here since the feature shipped\n-- and the table never existed, so `getRecentExposureSet` has always returned an\n-- empty set and every drill has picked as if the student had seen nothing.\n-- Verified absent in prod 2026-08-06.\n--\n-- Append-only, many rows per user: the read is "the most recent N qids", which\n-- needs the history, not a single current value. Deliberately NOT unique on\n-- (user_id, question_id) — a repeat serve is a real event worth recording, and\n-- a unique constraint would make recordExposures\' bulk insert fail the whole\n-- batch the first time a question came round again.\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.question_exposures (\n  id          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  user_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n  question_id text NOT NULL,\n  shown_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()\n);\n\n-- The only read is: this user\'s rows, newest first, limit N. Ordering the index\n-- descending on shown_at lets that come straight off the index.\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS question_exposures_user_recent_idx\n  ON public.question_exposures (user_id, shown_at DESC);\n\nALTER TABLE public.question_exposures ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\n\nDROP POLICY IF EXISTS "own exposures: select" ON public.question_exposures;\nCREATE POLICY "own exposures: select" ON public.question_exposures\n  FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (auth.uid() = user_id);\n\nDROP POLICY IF EXISTS "own exposures: insert" ON public.question_exposures;\nCREATE POLICY "own exposures: insert" ON public.question_exposures\n  FOR INSERT TO authenticated WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id);\n\n-- No UPDATE or DELETE policy: an exposure is a fact about what happened, and\n-- nothing in the app edits or removes one.\nGRANT SELECT, INSERT ON public.question_exposures TO authenticated;\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '-- Create table to track question usage per user\nCREATE TABLE public.question_usage (\n  id uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() PRIMARY KEY,\n  class_id text NOT NULL,\n  qid text NOT NULL,\n  last_seen_at timestamp with time zone NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  times_seen integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,\n  mode text NOT NULL, -- \'adaptive\', \'full-section\', \'type-drill\', \'natural-drill\'\n  created_at timestamp with time zone NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  UNIQUE(class_id, qid, mode)\n);\n\n-- Enable RLS\nALTER TABLE public.question_usage ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\n\n-- Users can view their own usage\nCREATE POLICY "Users can view own question usage"\nON public.question_usage\nFOR SELECT\nUSING (class_id = (auth.jwt() ->> \'class_id\'));\n\n-- Users can insert their own usage\nCREATE POLICY "Users can insert own question usage"\nON public.question_usage\nFOR INSERT\nWITH CHECK (class_id = (auth.jwt() ->> \'class_id\'));\n\n-- Users can update their own usage\nCREATE POLICY "Users can update own question usage"\nON public.question_usage\nFOR UPDATE\nUSING (class_id = (auth.jwt() ->> \'class_id\'));\n\n-- Users can delete their own usage (for reset)\nCREATE POLICY "Users can delete own question usage"\nON public.question_usage\nFOR DELETE\nUSING (class_id = (auth.jwt() ->> \'class_id\'));\n\n-- Create index for performance\nCREATE INDEX idx_question_usage_class_qid ON public.question_usage(class_id, qid, mode);\nCREATE INDEX idx_question_usage_last_seen ON public.question_usage(class_id, last_seen_at);'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '-- Create update timestamp function if it doesn\'t exist\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column()\nRETURNS TRIGGER AS $$\nBEGIN\n  NEW.updated_at = now();\n  RETURN NEW;\nEND;\n$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql SET search_path = public;\n\n-- Create Wrong Answer Journal table\nCREATE TABLE public.wrong_answer_journal (\n  id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  class_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  qid TEXT NOT NULL,\n  pt INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  section INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  qnum INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  qtype TEXT NOT NULL,\n  level INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  first_wrong_at_iso TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  last_status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (last_status IN (\'wrong\', \'right\')),\n  revisit_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  history_json JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT \'[]\'::jsonb,\n  created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),\n  updated_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),\n  UNIQUE(class_id, qid)\n);\n\n-- Enable RLS\nALTER TABLE public.wrong_answer_journal ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\n\n-- RLS Policies\nCREATE POLICY "Students can view own WAJ entries"\n  ON public.wrong_answer_journal\n  FOR SELECT\n  USING (true);\n\nCREATE POLICY "Students can insert own WAJ entries"\n  ON public.wrong_answer_journal\n  FOR INSERT\n  WITH CHECK (true);\n\nCREATE POLICY "Students can update own WAJ entries"\n  ON public.wrong_answer_journal\n  FOR UPDATE\n  USING (true);\n\n-- Index for faster lookups\nCREATE INDEX idx_waj_class_qid ON public.wrong_answer_journal(class_id, qid);\nCREATE INDEX idx_waj_class_last_status ON public.wrong_answer_journal(class_id, last_status);\nCREATE INDEX idx_waj_class_qtype ON public.wrong_answer_journal(class_id, qtype);\n\n-- Trigger to update updated_at\nCREATE TRIGGER update_waj_updated_at\n  BEFORE UPDATE ON public.wrong_answer_journal\n  FOR EACH ROW\n  EXECUTE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column();'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "-- Phase 2 (COMPREHENSIVEAUDIT): atomic counter RPCs.\ncreate or replace function public.increment_daily_stats(\n  p_class_id text,\n  p_date date,\n  p_xp integer,\n  p_time_ms bigint,\n  p_correct boolean\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  insert into daily_stats (class_id, date, questions_answered, correct_answers, xp_earned, time_spent_ms)\n  values (p_class_id, p_date, 1, case when p_correct then 1 else 0 end, coalesce(p_xp, 0), coalesce(p_time_ms, 0))\n  on conflict (class_id, date) do update set\n    questions_answered = daily_stats.questions_answered + 1,\n    correct_answers    = daily_stats.correct_answers + (case when p_correct then 1 else 0 end),\n    xp_earned          = daily_stats.xp_earned + coalesce(p_xp, 0),\n    time_spent_ms      = daily_stats.time_spent_ms + coalesce(p_time_ms, 0);\n$$;\n\ncreate or replace function public.increment_xp_total(\n  p_class_id text,\n  p_xp integer\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  update profiles\n     set xp_total = coalesce(xp_total, 0) + coalesce(p_xp, 0)\n   where class_id = p_class_id;\n$$;\n\ncreate or replace function public.increment_question_seen(\n  p_class_id text,\n  p_qid text,\n  p_mode text\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  insert into question_usage (class_id, qid, mode, times_seen, last_seen_at)\n  values (p_class_id, p_qid, p_mode, 1, now())\n  on conflict (class_id, qid, mode) do update set\n    times_seen   = question_usage.times_seen + 1,\n    last_seen_at = now();\n$$;\n\ncreate or replace function public.append_waj_wrong(\n  p_class_id text,\n  p_qid text,\n  p_item jsonb,\n  p_pt integer default null,\n  p_section integer default null,\n  p_qnum integer default null,\n  p_qtype text default null,\n  p_level integer default null\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  insert into wrong_answer_journal\n    (class_id, qid, pt, section, qnum, qtype, level, last_status, revisit_count, history_json)\n  values\n    (p_class_id, p_qid, p_pt, p_section, p_qnum, p_qtype, p_level, 'wrong', 0, jsonb_build_array(p_item))\n  on conflict (class_id, qid) do update set\n    history_json  = wrong_answer_journal.history_json || p_item,\n    last_status   = 'wrong',\n    revisit_count = jsonb_array_length(wrong_answer_journal.history_json),\n    updated_at    = now();\n$$;\n\ncreate or replace function public.append_waj_correct(\n  p_class_id text,\n  p_qid text,\n  p_item jsonb\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  update wrong_answer_journal set\n    history_json  = history_json || p_item,\n    last_status   = 'right',\n    revisit_count = jsonb_array_length(history_json),\n    updated_at    = now()\n  where class_id = p_class_id and qid = p_qid;\n$$;\n\ncreate or replace function public.increment_chapter_view(\n  p_topic_tag text,\n  p_section_id text\n) returns void\nlanguage sql\nsecurity invoker\nset search_path = public\nas $$\n  insert into chapter_progress (user_id, topic_tag, section_id, view_count, first_viewed_at, last_viewed_at)\n  values (auth.uid(), p_topic_tag, p_section_id, 1, now(), now())\n  on conflict (user_id, topic_tag, section_id) do update set\n    view_count     = chapter_progress.view_count + 1,\n    last_viewed_at = now();\n$$;\n\ngrant execute on function public.increment_daily_stats(text, date, integer, bigint, boolean) to authenticated, anon;\ngrant execute on function public.increment_xp_total(text, integer) to authenticated, anon;\ngrant execute on function public.increment_question_seen(text, text, text) to authenticated, anon;\ngrant execute on function public.append_waj_wrong(text, text, jsonb, integer, integer, integer, text, integer) to authenticated, anon;\ngrant execute on function public.append_waj_correct(text, text, jsonb) to authenticated, anon;\ngrant execute on function public.increment_chapter_view(text, text) to authenticated, anon;"}]

 xp_total int DEFAULT 0,\r\n  daily_goal_streak int DEFAULT 0,\r\n  updated_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()\r\n);\r\nALTER TABLE profiles ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can view profiles FOR UPDATE USING (true);\r\n\r\n-- Sessions table\r\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (\r\n  class_id text PRIMARY KEY,\r\n  queue_json jsonb DEFAULT \'[]\'::jsonb,\r\n  cursor_index int \'[]\'::jsonb,\r\n  cooldowns_json jsonb DEFAULT \'{}\'::jsonb,\r\n  markup_json jsonb DEFAULT \'{}\'::jsonb,\r\n  updated_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()\r\n);\r\nALTER TABLE sessions ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can view own session" ON sessions FOR SELECT USING (true);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can insert own session" ON sessions FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can update own session" ON sessions FOR UPDATE USING (true);\r\n\r\n-- Settings table\r\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS settings (\r\n  class_id text PRIMARY KEY,\r\n  enabled_qtypes text[] DEFAULT ARRAY[]::text[],\r\n  enabled_levels int[] DEFAULT ARRAY[1,2,3,4,5]::int[],\r\n  adaptive_on boolean DEFAULT true,\r\n  explore_ratio decimal DEFAULT 0.15,\r\n  pace_vs_challenge decimal DEFAULT 0,\r\n  time_pref text DEFAULT \'balanced\',\r\n  updated_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()\r\n);\r\nALTER TABLE settings ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can view own settings" ON settings FOR SELECT USING (true);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can insert own settings" ON settings FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can update own settings" ON settings FOR UPDATE USING (true);\r\n\r\n-- Events table\r\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (\r\n  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n  class_id text NOT NULL,\r\n  event text NOT events FOR SELECT USING (true);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can insert own events" ON events FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true);\r\n\r\n-- Wrong Answer Journal table\r\nCREATE TABLE public.wrong_answer_journal (\r\n  id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n  class_id TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n  qid TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n  pt INTEGER NOT NULL,\r\n  section INTEGER NOT NULL,\r\n  qnum INTEGER NOT NULL,\r\n  qtype TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n  level INTEGER NOT NULL,\r\n  first_wrong_at_iso TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\r\n  last_status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (last_status IN (\'wrong\', \'right\')),\r\n  revisit_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\r\n  history_json JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT \'[]\'::jsonb,\r\n  created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),\r\n  updated_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),\r\n  UNIQUE(class_id, qid)\r\n);\r\nALTER TABLE public.wrong_answer_journal ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nCREATE POLICY "Students can view own WAJ
